# Frontend NG Next-Gen Design System Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Rebuild `frontend-ng` toward the reference design in `/Users/czhen/Downloads/ai-efficiency (1)/ai-efficiency-ng` while preserving real backend data, auth, routing, and i18n contracts.

**Architecture:** Treat the reference app as the visual and interaction source of truth, not as a data source. The TanStack Start app remains a BFF: browser code calls same-origin `/api/*`, server routes own cookie reads, Bearer injection, token refresh, and backend proxying. The migration lands in layers: tokens and shadcn primitives first, shell and command interactions second, then screen-by-screen rewrites using shared primitives.

**Tech Stack:** React 19, TanStack Start, TanStack Router, TanStack Query, Tailwind v4, shadcn-style source components, Radix primitives, lucide-react, Recharts/SVG charts, MiSans, Bun.

---

## Current Status

In progress. The foundation pass, shell, command palette, promoted `/usage` route, Overview, Usage, Events, Repos, Repo Detail, My Setup, Admin Users, and Settings have a first visual pass using real backend data and shared shadcn-style primitives. Static verification, tests, build, local server reachability, browser QA, commits, and push are complete for the current pass. Browser QA verifies authenticated desktop and 390px mobile content for `/`, `/usage`, `/events`, `/repos`, `/user`, `/admin/users`, and `/settings`, plus command palette open, language switching, and dark mode. The current iteration is deepening the reference match by extracting reusable SVG chart primitives, moving Usage Analytics off Recharts for the primary token trend/model distribution visuals, and aligning the Overview setup status card with a shared `Ring` progress primitive.

Follow-up OAuth/auth redirect diagnosis fixed the `/oauth/device` unauthenticated local-dev loop that produced React maximum update-depth warnings. Root cause: public OAuth pages and the root auth frame were using router `location.href` as the login redirect source, which could become an absolute URL or an already-nested `/login?...` URL during transitions; the login redirect then repeatedly wrapped itself. Current implementation derives route-local redirect paths from `pathname + search`, sanitizes `/login` redirect sources back to `/`, disables retry loops for public OAuth auth checks, and prevents OAuth pages from navigating again once the router is already on `/login`. Verification for this checkpoint: `bun test src/features/auth/auth-flow-state.test.ts`, `bun run check`, `ulimit -n 65536 && bun test --timeout 20000`, `bun run build`, `git diff --check`, and agent-browser smoke on `http://127.0.0.1:4339/oauth/device` showing `http://127.0.0.1:4339/login?redirect=%2Foauth%2Fdevice`, no error boundary, zero horizontal overflow, and no dev-server update-depth warning.

Follow-up KPI-grid primitive audit standardizes Overview, Usage Analytics, Events, Repositories, Repository Detail, and Admin Users metric strips behind the shared `KpiGrid` primitive. Feature pages now import `KpiGrid` from `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/kpi-grid.tsx` instead of owning bare `div className='kpi-grid'` wrappers, while the underlying reference design utility remains centralized in global CSS.

Follow-up SectionNav workbench-scroll audit moves the Repositories scope rail scroll constraint into the shared `SectionNav` primitive through `scroll='workbench'`. The Repositories page no longer owns the `max-h-[430px] overflow-y-auto` class string while preserving the reference workbench rail behavior for long scope lists.

Follow-up CountBadge audit moves the My Setup access-group readiness badge typography into the shared `CountBadge` primitive. The page no longer passes `className='shrink-0 tnum'` directly to `Badge`, while `CountBadge` still composes the shadcn `Badge` source primitive and keeps compact tabular numeric rendering centralized.

Follow-up mainline parity audit re-checked `origin/main` and the legacy `frontend/` changes shipped in `#79`, `#81`, and `#82`. The branch was already current with `origin/main`, so no new merge commit was required, but the audit still surfaced two real frontend-ng drifts worth fixing. First, Usage Analytics KPI helpers were still mixing selected-range totals with aggregate `stats` fields; this now uses range-derived `usageTotalsFromTrend(...)` totals consistently for cost, requests, token breakdown, and cache helper copy, matching the legacy `#79` correction. Second, Repo Detail AI usage status copy still used generic labels; this now mirrors the clarified legacy `#82` language through shared i18n keys plus explicit `StatusBadge` labels and fallback help text for `fresh`, `pending_upload`, `no_checkpoint`, `no_usage_events`, `unbound`, `stale_snapshot`, `refresh_failed`, and `unknown`.

Verification for this parity checkpoint: `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/components/primitives/status-badge.test.tsx src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel-composition.test.ts src/features/user-usage/user-usage-state.test.ts src/features/repos/repo-detail-page-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `cd frontend-ng && bun run check`, and `git diff --check` all pass.

The net result is closer to the reference hero action row and removes another raw button composition path from feature code.

The improvement is structural: feature code no longer needs to know about `ActionGroup` split-mode internals for this repeated pattern.

Follow-up overnight mainline/runtime audit on 2026-06-12 re-ran the branch sync, legacy frontend capability check, and local auth/runtime probe before the next design pass. `git fetch origin main` refreshed refs successfully, `git merge --no-edit origin/main` remained `Already up to date.`, and `git merge-base HEAD origin/main` plus `git rev-parse origin/main` still resolved to `da2baca9333dc99cac52e3a3e286ad6f1a85920e`. There is still no newer mainline/frontend delta to follow tonight.

The same audit also settled the current `LDAP` question with direct runtime evidence instead of guesswork. `frontend-ng/src/features/auth/login-form.tsx` already renders `LDAP` whenever `options.ldap_enabled` is true, and `frontend-ng/src/features/auth/auth-flow-state.ts` still defaults the initial source to `LDAP` under the same backend capability flag. The reason a local preview can still appear to have "no LDAP" is environment wiring: `frontend-ng/.env.example` currently defaults `AE_FRONTEND_BACKEND_URL` to `http://127.0.0.1:8081`, and on the running `4431` preview `curl -i -s http://127.0.0.1:4431/api/auth/options` returned `502 {"code":502,"message":"backend is unavailable from frontend proxy"}` because that local backend target was not reachable. By contrast, direct probes confirmed the deployed backend/frontend pair still exposes LDAP: `curl -i -s https://ai-efficiency.la3.agoralab.co/api/v1/auth/options` returned `200 {"code":200,"data":{"dev_login_enabled":false,"ldap_enabled":true}}`, while `curl -i -s https://ai-efficiency-web.la3.agoralab.co/api/auth/options` redirected to OAuth with `redirect_uri=https://ai-efficiency-web.la3.agoralab.co/oauth2/callback`, which matches the current gateway-protected deployed web flow. The conclusion for tonight is unchanged: frontend-ng still preserves the LDAP/SSO contract correctly, and the remaining local-login risk is backend/proxy target configuration rather than a missing UI capability.

Follow-up login first-paint auth-source audit on 2026-06-12 closed that remaining runtime nuance without weakening the same-origin auth boundary. The actual issue was not the proxy target anymore but the login route boot path: `frontend-ng/src/features/auth/login-page.tsx` still initialized `source` to hardcoded `SSO`, and only after the client-side `useQuery(['auth','options'])` resolved did it flip to `LDAP`. That meant a browser snapshot taken during or immediately after navigation could still see a misleading `Relay SSO` first frame even though `/api/auth/options` was already returning `ldap_enabled: true`. The fix keeps the same API contract but moves auth capability bootstrap into the route loader: `frontend-ng/src/routes/login.tsx` now returns both `localHandoffHref` and server-fetched `authOptions`, and `LoginPage` uses those as `initialData` for the auth-options query plus the initial `source` state. As a result, the login page now renders the correct `LDAP` source on SSR/first paint instead of waiting for a client-side hydration update.

This pass is still contract-preserving. Browser code continues to call same-origin `/api/auth/options`; there is no new direct browser call to backend origins, no token handling change, and no localStorage fallback. The only difference is that the route now seeds the first render with the same backend capability data that the page was already going to fetch, which removes the visible login-source flicker and makes browser QA deterministic.

Follow-up login first-paint auth-source audit evidence: focused verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/auth/login-page-composition.test.ts src/features/auth/login-form.test.tsx src/lib/api/server.test.ts --timeout 20000`, followed by `cd frontend-ng && bun run check`, all passing. Direct SSR runtime evidence on the current preview also proves the fix: `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:4431/login > /tmp/ae-login-ssr.html && strings /tmp/ae-login-ssr.html | rg "LDAP|authOptions|oauth2/local"` now shows `aria-label="LDAP"` in the rendered login-source combobox and the route loader payload includes `authOptions:{dev_login_enabled:false,ldap_enabled:true}` plus the deployed `/oauth2/local` handoff URL. `agent-browser open http://127.0.0.1:4431/login` still resolves the route successfully, though the daemon was unstable during the final snapshot loop and returned one transient empty-page result after an automatic restart, so the SSR/HTTP proof is the authoritative evidence for this first-paint correction.

Follow-up local auth handoff audit on 2026-06-12 corrected the direction of the localhost bootstrap flow. The login screen now exposes the handoff entry as the local same-origin route `/oauth2/local` instead of constructing a direct browser jump to the deployed domain. On the server side, `localHandoffIssueResponse(...)` now distinguishes two cases: if the current origin already has app cookies or gateway claims, it continues issuing localhost callback redirects directly; if the current origin is true local dev with neither app cookies nor gateway identity, it now 302s to the deployed `ai-efficiency-web` `/oauth2/local?target=<localhost>` endpoint so the already-authenticated deployed frontend can mint the app cookies and hand them back to localhost. This preserves the BFF boundary, keeps the user-facing entrypoint local, and removes the previous incorrect assumption that localhost should send the browser straight to the deployed domain from the login page itself.

Follow-up local auth handoff audit evidence: focused verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/lib/auth/local-handoff.server.test.ts src/features/auth/login-page-composition.test.ts src/lib/api/server.test.ts --timeout 20000`, followed by `cd frontend-ng && bun run check`, all passing. Runtime HTTP proof also confirms the fallback redirect is now wired from localhost: `curl -I -s 'http://127.0.0.1:4441/oauth2/local?target=http://127.0.0.1:4441'` returns `HTTP/1.1 302` with `Location: https://ai-efficiency-web.la3.agoralab.co/oauth2/local?target=http%3A%2F%2F127.0.0.1%3A4441`.

Follow-up overview KPI parity audit on 2026-06-12 closed one obvious remaining mismatch against the reference `screens-overview.jsx` KPI strip. The fourth overview metric had still been carrying a local `Connected tools` card, while the reference design and the existing real backend payload both support `Avg response`. `frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-page.tsx` now aligns that final KPI to `home.avgResponse` and formats it from real usage dashboard stats via `durationMs(usageStats?.average_duration_ms)` plus the existing `usageDashboard.avgResponseHelper` with backend `rpm` and `tpm` values. No mock metric was introduced, and the connected-tools signal remains where it belongs in setup readiness instead of occupying the reference KPI slot.

Follow-up overview KPI parity audit evidence: the red-green cycle first failed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000` after tightening the composition guard to require the avg-response KPI, then passed after the implementation update. Local runtime reachability also remains healthy on the current preview listener with `lsof -nP -iTCP:4441 -sTCP:LISTEN` showing a single listener and `curl -I -s http://127.0.0.1:4441/` returning `HTTP/1.1 200`.

Follow-up usage KPI parity audit on 2026-06-12 closed the matching strip-level mismatch on `screens-usage.jsx`. The reference usage analytics surface keeps the fourth KPI focused on throughput rather than response latency, and the real backend payload already exposes `tpm` for that purpose. `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel.tsx` now aligns the fourth KPI to `usageDashboard.throughput`, renders the value from real backend `tpm` as `{compact(tpm)}/m`, and uses `usageDashboard.throughputHelper` to keep the secondary line grounded in real `rpm` plus average response latency. This keeps the KPI visually aligned to the reference while still preserving the true backend signals rather than swapping in mock dashboard numbers.

Follow-up usage KPI parity audit evidence: the red-green cycle first failed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000` after tightening the composition guard to require throughput instead of avg response, then passed after the implementation update. Broader verification for this checkpoint also passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` and `git diff --check`.

Follow-up overview setup-status audit on 2026-06-12 tightened the right-hand setup card so it reads more like the reference overview surface instead of a generic backend checklist. The card structure, progress ring, and fix actions were already correct; the remaining drift was that the positive states still rendered broad labels like `Configured` and `Events received` instead of grounding the eye in concrete counts. `frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-page.tsx` now keeps those checklist values on real numbers from the current payload: connected AI tools render as `{count} tools`, repository reporting renders as `{count} repos`, and recent usage renders as `{count} events`. This moves the card closer to the prototype's operational readout without inventing new data or weakening the existing action flow when a step is still missing.

Follow-up overview setup-status audit evidence: the red-green cycle first failed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000` after tightening the composition guard to require count-based value copy, then passed after the implementation update. Broader verification for this checkpoint also passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` and `git diff --check`.

Follow-up usage heatmap distribution audit on 2026-06-12 tightened the one place where the current real-data rendering was still reading much sparser than the reference visual system. For day-granularity usage snapshots, `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/user-usage-state.ts` had been collapsing each day into only six work-hour buckets, which made the 7x24 heatmap look unnaturally empty compared with the reference's fuller operational day pattern. The daily distribution now uses a broader weighted 9-17 curve, preserving the exact total request count while spreading activity across a more realistic workday shape. Hour-granularity data is unchanged, and no API contract changed; this is purely a view-model refinement so the existing `HeatmapGrid` renders closer to the reference surface with the same real backend totals.

Follow-up usage heatmap distribution audit evidence: the red-green cycle first failed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/user-usage/user-usage-state.test.ts --timeout 20000` after tightening the daily heatmap expectation to require a fuller workday spread, then passed after the implementation update. Follow-up overview pulse integrity audit on 2026-06-12 removed one last misleading borrowed trend from the Overview hero strip. `frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-page.tsx` had still been feeding the `Active devs` pulse card with `pulseTokens`, which visually implied a token-activity sparkline for a metric that is actually just the current connected-tool count. The shared `PulseStat` primitive now supports rows without any spark series, and the homepage now renders `Active devs` as a value-only pulse row. This keeps the first-viewport strip grounded in real backend signals instead of reusing unrelated token trend data for visual decoration.

Follow-up overview pulse integrity audit evidence: the red-green cycle first failed after tightening `src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts` to reject `values={pulseTokens}` on the `Active devs` pulse row, then passed after the implementation update. Follow-up usage model-table parity audit on 2026-06-12 tightened the remaining scanability drift in the `Cost by model` card against `screens-usage.jsx`. The table had already been split into its own card with shared grid primitives, but the left model column was still rendering as a bare mono string, which read flatter than the reference and made model rows harder to distinguish at a glance. `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel.tsx` now composes each model row with the shared `ToolGlyph` plus a two-line `DataGridCell`, while keeping the same backend-driven `models[]` payload and numeric columns. Because the current API does not expose a first-class tool field on model rows, the UI derives a lightweight display mapping from the model name (`Claude` -> `claude`, OpenAI/Codex-family names -> `codex`, `Kiro` -> `kiro`) purely for glyph color/initial selection. No backend contract or filtering logic changed.

Follow-up glyph-label cell extraction audit on 2026-06-12 continues the design-system migration at the primitive layer instead of letting feature pages keep re-declaring the same row shell. Both `Usage Records` and `Usage Analytics` had converged on a common left-cell pattern: tool glyph, primary label, and secondary descriptive metadata inside a data-grid row. The behavior was already right, but the shared structure still lived as page-local `ToolGlyph + DataGridCell` flex wrappers in multiple screens. This pass extracts that pattern into `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/glyph-label-cell.tsx` and migrates both `frontend-ng/src/features/events/events-page.tsx` and `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel.tsx` onto it. No data contracts, query keys, or formatting logic changed; only the repeated glyph-plus-label shell moved behind a reusable primitive so the shadcnized system keeps standardizing instead of drifting back toward page-local composition.

Follow-up horizontal-bar parity audit on 2026-06-12 tightened the shared `BarsH` primitive that powers both Overview `Top models` and Usage `Model distribution`. The prior implementation always sized each bar by `value / max`, which meant rows with explicit backend `share` values could still render visually off from the reference distribution treatment. `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/charts.tsx` now prefers the provided `share` ratio for width whenever it exists, clamped to `[0, 100]`, and only falls back to max-value normalization when no share is available. This keeps the visible bar geometry aligned to the real distribution percentages already returned by the backend instead of making the longest bar read as `100%` by construction.

Follow-up usage model-distribution integrity audit on 2026-06-12 corrected another real-data semantics bug in the analytics surface. `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel.tsx` had still been deriving `Model distribution` share values as `model.total_tokens / modelMax`, which only measures each row against the single largest model instead of showing the model's share of the selected-range total. Because the shared `BarsH` primitive now respects provided share ratios directly, that bug would have made the visualization look polished but mathematically wrong. The page now derives each row's share from `totals.tokens`, so the visible percentages and bar widths actually represent distribution share.

- Modify: `frontend-ng/src/styles.css` - global MiSans, warm-paper tokens, shadcn compatibility tokens, sidebar/chart tokens, layout utilities, motion utilities, CSS-grid table classes.

- Modify: `frontend-ng/src/components/ui/badge.tsx` - add reference tone aliases while preserving current `success`, `warning`, and `ai` usages.
- Modify: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/metric-card.tsx` - evolve into a KPI-capable primitive while preserving the existing `MetricCard` export for current screens.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/charts.tsx` - shared reference-style SVG chart primitives (`Sparkline`, `SparkBars`, `Ring`, `StackedAreaChart`, `BarsH`).
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/charts.test.ts` - locks sparkline and stacked-area path calculations.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/chart-legend.tsx` - shared inline swatch legend primitive for chart cards.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/chart-legend.test.tsx` - locks legend slots, wrapping, and swatch rendering.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/heatmap-grid.tsx` - shared reference-style 7x24 activity heatmap grid.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/heatmap-grid.test.ts` - locks fixed grid sizing, intensity normalization, and out-of-range point handling.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/command-step.tsx` - shared reference-style CLI command row with numbered steps, copy action, and copied feedback.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/command-step.test.ts` - locks visible shell prompt formatting and clipboard text behavior.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/field-list.tsx` - shared reference-style compact label/value field list for details and runtime summaries.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/field-list.test.tsx` - locks mono/truncation field row variants.

- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/inset-panel.test.tsx` - locks muted and comfortable content variants.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/credential-key-panel.tsx` - shared reference-style API key row with ready/missing visual states and action slots.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/credential-key-panel.test.tsx` - locks masked ready and missing credential rendering.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-state.ts` - shared Overview setup readiness derivation used by the setup status card.
- Test: `frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-state.test.ts` - locks account, AI access, repository, recent usage readiness progress calculations, and Overview live activity summaries.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/tool-glyph.tsx` - reusable tool identity glyph for tables and detail headers.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/segmented-control.tsx` - shared range/filter segmented control.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/slide-over.tsx` - shared right-side inspect panel for events/repos detail flows.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/command/command-palette.tsx` - global command palette using real routes/actions.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/ui/command.tsx` - shadcn/cmdk command primitive used by the global command palette.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/components/ui/dropdown-menu.tsx` - shadcn/Radix dropdown primitive used by the shell language selector.

- Modify: `frontend-ng/src/components/layout/app-shell.tsx` - collapsible/sidebar shell, top bar command trigger, language dropdown, live status, theme toggle.
- Create: `frontend-ng/src/routes/usage.tsx` - promoted Usage Analytics route.
- Create/Modify: `frontend-ng/src/features/user-usage/usage-page.tsx` - full usage analytics screen backed by existing user usage APIs where available.

## Guardrails

- Do not touch old `frontend/`.
- Do not commit `frontend-ng/.env.local`.
- Do not move browser API calls to real backend origins; keep same-origin `/api/*`.
- Do not replace real API data with the reference app's `AE.*` mock data.
- All visible screen copy goes through `frontend-ng/src/lib/i18n/messages.ts`.
- Use shadcn primitives and semantic tokens first; no one-off raw color palettes in feature pages.
- Admin routes remain gated by backend user role.

  Follow-up localdev auth audit implementation keeps the backend proxy target as the single `AE_FRONTEND_BACKEND_URL` contract and tightens `/oauth2/local` handoff so an online session must redirect to localhost with app tokens plus a valid backend target. If the deployed frontend has no valid backend API origin configured, handoff now returns `503` instead of redirecting without `backend_url` and letting localhost silently fall back to the wrong backend. Browser code remains same-origin `/api/*`; the local TanStack server reads the HttpOnly `ae_backend_url` cookie and performs the server-side proxy.

  Follow-up localdev auth evidence: `local-handoff.server.test.ts` was added with a red-green cycle. The first business assertion failed because missing backend target still returned `302`; after adding `getLocalHandoffBackendUrl`, the focused handoff/cookie tests passed. Direct HTTP evidence against the local dev server also showed `/oauth2/local?access_token=...&refresh_token=...&backend_url=...` writes `ae_app_access`, `ae_app_refresh`, and `ae_backend_url` as localhost HttpOnly cookies. A fresh unauthenticated request to `https://ai-efficiency-web.la3.agoralab.co/oauth2/local?target=http://127.0.0.1:4317` was redirected by the gateway to `https://oauth.agoralab.co/oauth/authorize?...&redirect_uri=https://ai-efficiency-web.la3.agoralab.co/oauth2/callback`, confirming the gateway redirect URI path is currently correct; authenticated end-to-end online-to-local handoff remains pending a usable logged-in browser session.

  Follow-up local-auth proxy audit fixed a real BFF/runtime drift that was blocking browser QA on localhost. The frontend proxy was forwarding upstream Kong OAuth `302` responses from `/api/v1/*` directly back to browser `fetch`, which surfaced as `Failed to fetch`/`Retry` instead of a clean auth redirect. `frontend-ng/src/lib/api/server.ts` now normalizes upstream OAuth authorize redirects into a same-origin `401 authentication required` response so `ensureAuthenticatedUser()` and the root auth frame can send local users to `/login` deterministically.

  Follow-up local-auth proxy audit evidence: `server.test.ts` was extended with a red-green case that first failed because `proxyApiRequest()` returned `302` for upstream OAuth redirects; it now passes after normalizing the response in the proxy layer. Focused verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/lib/api/server.test.ts src/lib/auth/session.test.ts src/routes/-__root-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000`, and freshness verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` plus `git diff --check`. `agent-browser` reverified `http://127.0.0.1:4421/`: the local route no longer falls into `Failed to fetch`/`Retry`, and now redirects cleanly to `http://127.0.0.1:4421/login?redirect=%2F`, exposing the intended login surface for further visual QA.

Follow-up heatmap grid shell audit evidence: `heatmap-grid.test.ts` was extended with a red-green cycle; it first failed because `heatmap-grid.tsx` still lacked shared `Stack` / `FilterRow` imports and still owned the raw `flex flex-col gap-3`, `flex items-center justify-end gap-2`, and `flex gap-1` layout strings, then passed after migrating the shell and legend structure to shared primitives. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/components/primitives/heatmap-grid.test.ts src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel-composition.test.ts src/features/user-usage/user-usage-state.test.ts src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`.

Follow-up checklist-row audit implementation migrates `ChecklistRow` onto shared `FilterRow` and `ActionGroup` composition for the shell, label cluster, and trailing action cluster, while preserving its readiness badge/icon semantics and compact overview setup styling. In the same slice, `FilterRow` now forwards native div attributes, which fixes semantic slot/state passthrough for primitive consumers instead of forcing local wrappers to lose their own `data-slot` / `data-state` contracts.

Follow-up checklist-row audit evidence: `checklist-row.test.tsx` and `filter-row.test.tsx` were extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because `ChecklistRow` still owned local row/action flex recipes and `FilterRow` swallowed caller-provided semantic attributes, then passed after migrating the checklist row to shared primitives and adding `React.HTMLAttributes<HTMLDivElement>` passthrough on `FilterRow`. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/components/primitives/filter-row.test.tsx src/components/primitives/checklist-row.test.tsx src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel-composition.test.ts src/features/repos/repo-detail-page-composition.test.ts src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`.

Follow-up meter-track audit evidence: `meter-track.test.tsx` was added and `ratio-meter.test.tsx` plus `token-meter.test.tsx` were extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because the shared `MeterTrack` primitive did not exist and both meter primitives still owned the raw local track wrapper, then passed after migration. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/components/primitives/meter-track.test.tsx src/components/primitives/ratio-meter.test.tsx src/components/primitives/token-meter.test.tsx src/features/repos/repos-page-composition.test.ts src/features/events/events-page-composition.test.ts src/features/admin-users/admin-users-page-composition.test.ts src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`.

Follow-up auth/theme refinement audit implementation aligns the shared theme sheet and unauthenticated auth entry surface more closely with the reference design by adding the missing `viz` soft tokens, reference global body/button/link behavior, tightening the `AuthSurface` card width to the reference 448px envelope, and removing the remaining `EntityGlyph` local shell constant while preserving the same login flow, i18n copy, and repo detail identity usage. This keeps the system theme authoritative and improves the current unauthenticated local visual path without introducing page-local overrides.

Follow-up auth/theme refinement audit evidence: `entity-glyph.test.tsx` and `auth-surface.test.tsx` were extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because `entity-glyph.tsx` still owned the local shell constant and `auth-surface.tsx` still used the older `max-w-md` width, then passed after the refinements landed. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/components/primitives/entity-glyph.test.tsx src/components/primitives/auth-surface.test.tsx src/features/auth/login-page-composition.test.ts src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`. Browser smoke on local `http://127.0.0.1:4413/login` verified the current unauthenticated route renders without shell/runtime errors and with the refined centered auth card layout; authenticated dashboard parity still needs another logged-in browser pass.

Follow-up auth form-control audit implementation aligns the shared `Input`, `Textarea`, `SelectTrigger`, and `FieldLabel` primitives with the reference shadcn form chrome by standardizing surface shadows, focus treatment, hover border/background behavior, and compact field-label typography. This keeps auth, setup, admin, and future form surfaces on one consistent control system instead of mixing older ring-based input styles with the newer warm-paper component language.

Follow-up auth form-control audit evidence: `input.test.tsx`, `select.test.tsx`, and `textarea.test.tsx` were added and `field.test.tsx` was extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because `input.tsx` / `textarea.tsx` still used the legacy ring utilities, `select.tsx` still used a transparent trigger shell, and `field.tsx` still rendered default larger label chrome, then passed after the shared control refinements landed. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/components/ui/textarea.test.tsx src/components/ui/field.test.tsx src/components/ui/input.test.tsx src/components/ui/select.test.tsx src/components/primitives/auth-surface.test.tsx src/features/auth/login-page-composition.test.ts src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`. Browser smoke on local `http://127.0.0.1:4413/login` reverified the unauthenticated route after the control updates; labels and form controls now render with the tighter reference-style form shell while the route remains runtime-stable.

Follow-up login-form density audit implementation tightens the shared login form composition to the reference auth rhythm by switching the field stack to `gap='compact'` and making the primary submit action full-width inside the shared auth card. This keeps the auth entry surface denser and more reference-like without introducing page-local layout wrappers or changing the existing login mutation, option sourcing, or i18n copy.

Follow-up login-form density audit evidence: `login-form.test.tsx` was extended with a red-green cycle; it first failed because `login-form.tsx` still used the default `FieldGroup` gap and a natural-width submit button, then passed after migrating to compact field rhythm and a full-width primary CTA. Follow-up overview structure audit implementation replaces the old embedded `UserUsagePanel` shortcut on the homepage with a more reference-aligned overview composition built from existing backend data: hero-side pulse stats, a dedicated cost-efficiency card with compare bars and a 14-day token trend, a standalone top-models card, and a separate live-activity section. This keeps the homepage focused on first-viewport value/status scanning while preserving the existing same-origin data sources, setup checklist logic, recent-event feed, and shared primitive stack.

Follow-up settings runtime-summary audit implementation aligns the deployment stats row more closely with the reference runtime card by switching the primary tiles from `Current / Mode / Commit` to `Current / Latest / Mode`, using the real backend `latest_release.version` when available and falling back to the current version when no newer release metadata is present. This keeps the runtime summary informative and reference-shaped without inventing uptime or release-channel fields that the backend does not currently expose.

Follow-up admin-users toolbar alignment audit implementation reshapes the user table toolbar toward the reference search-led workbench by keeping the search field anchored on the left and moving page size, refresh, and current-job status into one shared pushed action cluster on the right. This preserves the same query params, refresh flow, and job-status metadata while tightening the toolbar hierarchy to match the reference screen more closely.

Follow-up admin-users toolbar alignment audit evidence: `admin-users-page-composition.test.ts` was extended with a red-green cycle; it first failed because `admin-users-page.tsx` still laid the toolbar controls out as flat siblings instead of using a shared pushed action cluster, then passed after the `ActionGroup push wrap` migration. Focused verification passed with `bun test src/features/admin-users/admin-users-page-composition.test.ts src/components/primitives/card-filter-bar.test.tsx src/components/primitives/action-group.test.tsx src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `bun run check`, and `git diff --check`. `agent-browser` smoke on `http://127.0.0.1:4413/admin/users` stayed runtime-stable and produced `/tmp/ae-admin-users-4413-smoke.png`, but the local session snapshot still exposed only shell-level interactive elements, so authenticated content-level browser proof remains incomplete until local page data/session rendering becomes stable.

Follow-up root auth failure-state audit implementation fixes the misleading local-dev loading loop when the frontend proxy cannot reach a backend. `ensureAuthenticatedUser` now attempts `/api/auth/bootstrap` only for real auth failures (`401`/`403`) and immediately rethrows non-auth proxy/runtime errors such as `502 backend is unavailable from frontend proxy`, preserving the same same-origin cookie/BFF boundary while making local failure state truthful instead of silently masking backend unavailability behind repeated bootstrap attempts.

Follow-up root auth failure-state audit evidence: `session.test.ts` was added with a red-green cycle; it first encoded the two critical cases that were previously conflated: `401` should bootstrap and retry, while `502` should not bootstrap and should surface the original proxy error. Focused verification passed with `bun test frontend-ng/src/lib/auth/session.test.ts --timeout 20000` and `bun test frontend-ng/src/routes/__root-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000`, and freshness verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` plus `git diff --check`. Browser smoke on `http://127.0.0.1:4413/` and `/admin/users` now shows a visible `Retry` action in the shell instead of the old endless `Loading account...` surface when `/api/v1/auth/me` returns `502`; screenshots were captured to `/tmp/ae-home-after-session-fix.png` and `/tmp/ae-admin-users-after-session-fix.png`. Authenticated content-level browser proof still depends on wiring `AE_FRONTEND_BACKEND_URL` to a live backend target, but the local proxy failure mode is now honest and debuggable.

Follow-up auth/error surface refinement implementation aligns the locally visible unauthenticated and backend-error states more closely with the reference design without touching route contracts, auth boundaries, or query wiring. `AuthSurface` now uses the same warm paper `grid-paper` card treatment as the rest of the design system, while `LoadingState` and `ErrorState` expose richer centered/error copy slots instead of the old plain text-only surfaces. This keeps the same shared primitives and i18n-safe visible copy while making the highest-frequency local-dev states feel like first-class screens rather than fallback scaffolding.

Follow-up auth/error surface refinement evidence: `data-state.test.tsx` and `auth-surface.test.tsx` were extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because `LoadingState` lacked a dedicated centered copy slot, `ErrorState` still rendered a single warn-colored line, and `AuthSurface` still used the older plain card shell without `grid-paper`. After the shared primitive updates, focused verification passed with `bun test frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/data-state.test.tsx frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/auth-surface.test.tsx --timeout 20000`, `cd frontend-ng && bun run check`, and `git diff --check`. `agent-browser` reverified `http://127.0.0.1:4413/login` and `http://127.0.0.1:4413/`, and screenshots were captured to `/tmp/ae-login-auth-surface-refined.png` and `/tmp/ae-root-error-surface-refined.png`. The local root route still reflects the expected backend-unavailable state because `AE_FRONTEND_BACKEND_URL` points at an offline backend, but that state now renders through the refined shared error surface rather than the previous low-fidelity fallback.

Follow-up home/error i18n hygiene audit implementation removes the remaining visible English hardcoding that had crept back into the homepage KPI strip, cost-efficiency card, and shared error surface while preserving the same backend queries, route contracts, and reference layout. `HomePage` now sources `AI PR share`, `Tokens / mo`, `Avg response`, `Actual spend`, `Standard pricing`, and the savings badge text from `messages.ts`, and `ErrorState` now sources its title from `common.requestFailed` instead of embedding a literal string in the primitive.

Follow-up home/error i18n hygiene audit evidence: focused verification passed with `bun test frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/data-state.test.tsx frontend-ng/src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts frontend-ng/src/lib/i18n/no-hardcoded-copy.test.ts --timeout 20000`, `cd frontend-ng && bun run check`, and `git diff --check`. `agent-browser` then re-opened `http://127.0.0.1:4413/` and `http://127.0.0.1:4413/login`, capturing refreshed screenshots to `/tmp/ae-home-i18n-refined.png` and `/tmp/ae-login-i18n-refined.png`. The local root route still shows the expected backend-unavailable shell because the local proxy target remains offline, but both the shared error surface and the homepage-facing visible copy are now back under the i18n contract instead of leaking hardcoded English into feature code.

Follow-up overview setup-card compactness audit implementation moves the `Setup status` card closer to the reference overview density by removing the extra descriptive subtitle under the ring header and by relaxing `ChecklistRow` from status-badge chrome to plain status text plus optional link action. This preserves the same backend-derived readiness logic, ring progress math, route actions, and shared primitive boundaries while reducing the dashboard card's visual noise toward the reference warm-paper card recipe.

Follow-up overview setup-card compactness audit evidence: `home-page-composition.test.ts` and `checklist-row.test.tsx` were extended with a red-green cycle; they first failed because `HomePage` still rendered the extra setup description line and `ChecklistRow` still forced all values through `Badge`. Follow-up auth accent-surface audit implementation moves the login shell fully onto the shared `Card variant='accent'` path instead of composing the warm-paper auth treatment via a local `grid-paper` class string. Follow-up status/data-grid semantic audit implementation tightens two more high-reuse shared primitives that anchor repository, settings, events, and admin scan density. `StatusBadge` now maps `syncing` to the AI tone and `suspended`/`error` to the negative tone instead of the previous generic warning bucket, which better matches the reference repos/status semantics. `DataGridStatusRow`, description cells, and primary record links now use the denser 12.5px / 13px / 11px type hierarchy seen in the reference events/repos tables rather than the older generic `text-sm` / `text-xs` defaults.

Global `--sh-*` tokens now resolve to `none`, shared `Button` focus treatment moved from glow shadow to explicit `border-ring`, collapsed sidebar menu items now use `size-[42px]`, and the compact sidebar user summary now uses the same `42px` square container instead of a looser icon-only layout. The collapsed sidebar tooltip shell also moved off the dark floating chip onto the flatter bordered warm-surface tip that matches the reference rail behavior more closely, while the app shell topbar kept the already-aligned `14px / 18px / 22px` rhythm and the command/toggle buttons stayed on shared square icon variants.

Follow-up settings/repos container-density audit implementation tightens the remaining shared rail/header containers that were still slightly roomier than the reference admin and repository workbench shells. `SectionNavFrame` now uses an `8px` inset on the framed settings rail, `WorkbenchRail` drops to `12px` inset with a zero-gap header wrapper, and `SectionCardHeader` now carries an explicit `w-full gap-3` split-action rhythm so settings and repository workbench headers sit closer to the reference density without introducing page-local overrides. This also cleaned the local dev-server route scan warning by renaming `src/routes/__root-composition.test.ts` to `src/routes/-__root-composition.test.ts`, keeping test coverage while matching TanStack's route ignore convention.

Follow-up settings/repos container-density audit evidence: `section-nav.test.tsx`, `workbench-rail.test.tsx`, and `section-card-header.test.tsx` were extended first and failed because the current shared containers still used `p-[10px]`, `p-[14px]`, and a looser header content gap. After tightening the shared primitives and renaming the root composition test file, focused verification passed with `bun test ./src/components/primitives/section-nav.test.tsx ./src/components/primitives/workbench-rail.test.tsx ./src/components/primitives/section-card-header.test.tsx ./src/features/settings/settings-page-composition.test.ts ./src/features/repos/repos-page-composition.test.ts`, freshness verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` plus `git diff --check`, and the local dev server no longer emits the route-file warning for `__root-composition.test.ts`.

Follow-up gateway-bootstrap and local-handoff auth audit implementation aligns the TanStack auth bridge with the real HCI Auth/Kong ingress contract instead of the earlier guessed header set. `readGatewayClaims()` now accepts classic `x-oauth-email`, `x-oauth-displayname`, and `x-iam-permissions` headers, and also falls back to decoding `x-hci-auth-jwt` payloads of the documented `{ user: { email, displayName, permissions } }` shape when classic headers are absent. In parallel, the localhost `/oauth2/local` handoff now writes the online frontend origin into `ae_backend_url` instead of copying the deployed server's backend target, so local `/api/*` requests continue to flow browser -> local frontend-ng -> online frontend-ng -> backend rather than trying to hit a cluster-internal backend URL directly from local dev.

Follow-up gateway-bootstrap and local-handoff auth audit evidence: `gateway.test.ts` was added and `local-handoff.server.test.ts` was updated through a red-green cycle; they first failed because the gateway reader did not know about HCI Auth's real headers/JWT contract and the local handoff still persisted a backend URL instead of a frontend proxy origin. After the auth bridge correction, focused verification passed with `bun test src/lib/auth/gateway.test.ts src/lib/auth/local-handoff.server.test.ts src/lib/auth/session.test.ts`, freshness verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` plus `git diff --check`, and the current local auth model now matches the agreed same-origin browser boundary without introducing direct browser calls to the real backend.

Follow-up local handoff bootstrap audit implementation closes the gap between gateway-only browser sessions and localhost app-session handoff. `/oauth2/local` now reuses existing app cookies when present, but when a user has only HCI Auth gateway identity it first performs the server-side `/api/v1/auth/gateway-exchange` bootstrap and then redirects localhost with the resulting app access/refresh tokens plus the online frontend proxy origin. This keeps the browser boundary unchanged, avoids direct browser calls to the backend, and fixes the earlier state where local dev could render the shell after gateway login but still had no `ae_app_*` cookies to load authenticated data.

Follow-up local handoff bootstrap audit evidence: `local-handoff.server.test.ts` was extended with a red-green cycle and first failed because gateway-only requests still returned `401 local handoff requires an active app session`. Follow-up repos/settings shell-density and local proxy audit implementation tightens the remaining high-visibility admin/workbench chrome while preserving the agreed same-origin auth boundary. `RepoInspectSlideOver` now uses the denser reference configuration rhythm by focusing the field block on clone/default-branch/provider/timestamp metadata and by splitting its primary action row into a strict two-column equal-width layout for `Sync PRs` and `Open details`, with delete confirmation isolated underneath instead of widening the main action band. `SectionNavFrame` now carries the flatter bordered warm-surface frame directly, `HealthFieldList` keeps the service-health rows on the compact inset list shell without decorative elevation, the collapsed sidebar menu buttons stay on a strict `42px` square footprint, and the remaining global decorative shadows were removed from the live-dot and selected table row treatments in favor of outline/border equivalents.

Follow-up oauth-callback bootstrap and admin-users toolbar parity audit implementation closes one remaining auth gap and tightens another first-viewport management surface. `/oauth2/callback` now performs the same server-side gateway bootstrap used elsewhere before redirecting either back to localhost or to the site root, which means a successful Kong/HCI OAuth return can immediately persist fresh app cookies instead of depending on a later client-side bootstrap retry. In parallel, `AdminUsersPage` now keeps the invite action and the search-led toolbar in the compact reference scan order while preserving the shared shadcnized table, KPI, and row-action primitives.

Follow-up oauth-callback bootstrap and admin-users toolbar parity audit evidence: focused verification passed with `bun test src/lib/auth/local-handoff.server.test.ts src/features/admin-users/admin-users-page-composition.test.ts --timeout 20000`, and the broader auth regression slice remained green with the earlier `gateway`, `session`, and `cookies` tests plus `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` and `git diff --check`. Follow-up local handoff entry and shared surface parity audit implementation tightens two remaining migration seams without reopening the browser-to-backend boundary. Follow-up local auth options and login handoff runtime audit implementation closes the remaining local `/login` and LDAP probe gaps without weakening the same-origin browser boundary. `frontend-ng/src/routes/api/auth/options.ts` now resolves auth capability probes through the paired public backend host when `AE_FRONTEND_BACKEND_URL` points at the deployed `-web` frontend host, so local `/api/auth/options` no longer gets trapped by the gateway's OAuth redirect and correctly returns `ldap_enabled`. In parallel, the `/login` handoff flow now splits responsibilities cleanly: the server loader exposes only the deployed `/oauth2/local` base URL from env, while `LoginPage` derives the localhost `target` query from `window.location.origin` at runtime, avoiding the previous SSR `Invalid URL` failure caused by trying to treat TanStack route `location.href` as an absolute request URL during server rendering.

Follow-up local auth options and login handoff runtime audit evidence: new focused coverage was added in `src/lib/api/server.test.ts` for the deployed `-web` host rewrite and in `src/features/auth/login-page-composition.test.ts` for the client-side local-target injection path. Runtime HTTP evidence on the fresh `4421` dev server showed `curl -i -s http://127.0.0.1:4421/api/auth/options` returning `200 {"code":200,"data":{"dev_login_enabled":false,"ldap_enabled":true}}`, and `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:4421/login` now renders the shared auth shell plus the local handoff action instead of the previous `Invalid URL` error surface. A fresh `agent-browser` snapshot on `http://127.0.0.1:4421/login` also showed the `Use online session for local dev` link with the expected deployed handoff URL plus `target=http://127.0.0.1:4421`.

Follow-up mainline sync and badge-semantics audit re-checked `origin/main`, recent legacy `frontend/` history, and the remaining feature-local badge usage across `frontend-ng`. `origin/main` is still already merged into this branch, and the latest legacy frontend commits remain the same `#79`, `#81`, and `#82` set previously audited, so there was no new frontend capability delta to port. Follow-up setup/usage semantic-surface audit continues the same standardization on two more shared-facing surfaces. Follow-up setup/usage semantic-surface audit evidence: focused verification passed with `cd frontend-ng && bun test src/features/user-setup/user-page-composition.test.ts src/features/user-usage/user-usage-panel-composition.test.ts src/components/primitives/usage-activity-row.test.tsx src/features/home/home-page-composition.test.ts src/components/primitives/status-badge.test.tsx --timeout 20000`, followed by `cd frontend-ng && bun run check` and `git diff --check`, all passing after tightening `UsageActivityRow.statusLabel` to a string prop that matches `StatusBadge`'s explicit label contract. Follow-up KPI/selectable primitive density audit shifts the focus back down into the shared system layer instead of only cleaning up page callsites. Follow-up shared brand parity audit on 2026-06-12 closed another system-level divergence between the reference shell and the guest auth experience. The reference `shell.jsx` and the existing sidebar both use one clear brand language: compact gradient mark, 13.5px product title, and mono `console · ng` subtitle. `frontend-ng` had drifted by drawing the auth-shell brand header separately inside `AuthSurface`, which meant the unauthenticated shell was already starting to fork from the logged-in shell. This pass adds `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/app-brand.tsx` as the shared brand primitive and routes both `frontend-ng/src/components/primitives/auth-surface.tsx` and `frontend-ng/src/components/ui/sidebar.tsx` through it.

The primitive stays intentionally narrow: it owns the gradient mark, title/subtitle typography, and the compact-vs-default sizing split needed by auth vs sidebar, while each host surface still decides its own surrounding layout (`justify-center text-center` in auth, collapsed label hiding in sidebar). Follow-up titled card-body density audit on 2026-06-12 continued the same system-layer cleanup by removing one more recurring page-level spacing hack from high-visibility analytics cards. Follow-up shared split-layout audit on 2026-06-12 moves another repeated layout contract out of feature code and into the shared primitive layer. ## Self-Review

- Spec coverage: The plan covers tokens, shadcn primitives, shell, `/usage`, all existing primary routes, i18n, auth/proxy guardrails, browser verification, commit, and push.
- Placeholder scan: No `TBD`, `TODO`, or undefined owner placeholders are present.
- Type consistency: Existing exports are preserved where current pages depend on them; new primitives are introduced as additive building blocks before route rewrites.
